  <div class="ff-about-hero">
    <span class="label">About FicForge</span>
    <h1>A home for fanfiction, original fiction, and serialized storytelling.</h1>
    <p>
      FicForge is a story-first platform for readers, fanfiction writers, original webnovel authors, and creators building worlds one chapter at a time. It is designed to bring reading, publishing, community, and creator support together in one place.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-about-actions">
      <a class="primary" href="/browse/">Start Reading</a>
      <a href="/write/">Start Writing</a>
      <a href="/community/">Join Community</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-about-note">
    <strong>Stories come first.</strong>
    <p>
      FicForge is not being built as a forum with stories attached. It is being built as a fiction platform where discussion, support, and community grow naturally around the stories people read and create.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-section-head">
    <span class="label">What FicForge Is</span>
    <h2>A platform for readers and creators</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge exists to give writers a place to publish their work and give readers a place to discover fandom stories, original webnovels, serialized fiction, and creator-driven worlds.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-about-grid">
    <div class="ff-about-card">
      <span>Fanfiction</span>
      <h3>Fandom storytelling</h3>
      <p>
        FicForge welcomes transformative fiction, fandom-inspired stories, crossovers, alternate universes, canon divergence, and reader communities built around shared fictional worlds.
      </p>
    </div>

    <div class="ff-about-card">
      <span>Originals</span>
      <h3>Original webnovels</h3>
      <p>
        FicForge also supports original fiction, creator-owned stories, serialized novels, fantasy, sci-fi, romance, drama, action, dark fiction, and long-form webnovel projects.
      </p>
    </div>

    <div class="ff-about-card">
      <span>Community</span>
      <h3>Discussion around stories</h3>
      <p>
        Readers and writers can connect through story comments, forum rooms, recommendations, writing help, fandom discussions, and community spaces centered on fiction.
      </p>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-about-panel">
    <span class="label">Our Vision</span>
    <h2>FicForge is built around the reader-writer connection</h2>
    <p>
      A strong fiction platform should make it easy for creators to publish and easy for readers to find stories worth following. FicForge is being built to support that connection through clean story pages, community interaction, creator profiles, memberships, Sparks, and future tools for original fiction monetization.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-about-two">
      <div class="ff-about-box">
        <strong>For readers</strong>
        <p>
          FicForge gives readers a place to browse stories, follow creators, join discussions, save favorites, support creators through approved features, and become part of growing story communities.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-about-box">
        <strong>For writers</strong>
        <p>
          FicForge gives writers a place to publish chapters, organize story details, build a public creator presence, connect with readers, and prepare for stronger creator tools as the platform grows.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-about-panel">
    <span class="label">Platform Values</span>
    <h2>What FicForge stands for</h2>

    <div class="ff-about-list">
      <div class="ff-about-row">
        <strong>Story-first design</strong>
        <p>
          FicForge is focused on reading, writing, publishing, discovery, and creator identity before anything else.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-about-row">
        <strong>Room for fanfiction and original work</strong>
        <p>
          Fanfiction and original fiction belong on the same platform, but they require different rules, rights boundaries, and monetization limits.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-about-row">
        <strong>Clear creator boundaries</strong>
        <p>
          FicForge does not sell fanfiction. Fanfiction chapters remain free to read and cannot be locked, sold, Fast Passed, or offered as paid early access.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-about-row">
        <strong>Useful community interaction</strong>
        <p>
          FicForge encourages discussion, feedback, recommendations, writing help, fandom talk, and reader activity without letting the community side overwhelm the story platform.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-about-row">
        <strong>Growth in stages</strong>
        <p>
          FicForge is being built step by step, starting with the foundation: pages, publishing, browsing, accounts, community, support, memberships, Sparks, and creator tools.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-about-panel">
    <span class="label">Creator Support</span>
    <h2>Supporting creators responsibly</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge is designed to support creators while respecting the difference between original fiction and fanfiction. Original webnovels may be eligible for broader monetization features as the platform grows. Fanfiction support must remain general profile support, not payment for specific fanfiction chapters, fandoms, updates, continuations, or rewards.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-about-two">
      <div class="ff-about-box">
        <strong>Sparks</strong>
        <p>
          Sparks are FicForge’s platform currency. Readers can use Sparks through approved features, including general creator support and future platform tools.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-about-box">
        <strong>Premium</strong>
        <p>
          Premium memberships give readers monthly Sparks, an ad-free experience, supporter status, profile perks, priority support, and early access to new FicForge features.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-about-panel">
    <span class="label">What We Are Building Toward</span>
    <h2>The long-term FicForge goal</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge is being built to become a home for serialized fiction, fandom storytelling, creator-owned webnovels, story communities, and reader-supported creativity. The platform will grow over time with better publishing tools, improved discovery, creator profiles, story organization, community features, and responsible monetization systems.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-about-panel">
    <span class="label">Explore FicForge</span>
    <h2>Where to go next</h2>
    <p>
      Start reading, begin writing, join the community, or review the support pages that explain how FicForge works.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-about-links">
      <a href="/browse/">Browse Stories</a>
      <a href="/write/">Write on FicForge</a>
      <a href="/community/">Community</a>
      <a href="/forum/">Forums</a>
      <a href="/premium/">Premium</a>
      <a href="/sparks-faq/">Sparks FAQ</a>
      <a href="/support/">Support Hub</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-about-bottom">
    <h2>FicForge is being forged for stories that keep growing.</h2>
    <p>
      Whether you are here to read, write, discuss, recommend, support, or build an audience, FicForge is designed to become a platform where stories come first and communities form around them.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-about-actions">
      <a class="primary" href="/browse/">Start Reading</a>
      <a href="/write/">Start Writing</a>
      <a href="/community/">Join Community</a>
    </div>
  </div>
</section>

    (function () {
      const params = new URLSearchParams(window.location.search);
      const key = params.get("rp_key") || params.get("key") || "";
      const login = params.get("rp_login") || params.get("login") || "";

      const keyField = document.getElementById("ff-rp-key");
      const loginField = document.getElementById("ff-rp-login");

      if (keyField) keyField.value = key;
      if (loginField) loginField.value = login;
    })();
  </script>

PAGE: Content Policy  (slug: content-policy)
================================================================================

<section class="ff-content-page">
  <div class="ff-content-hero">
    <span>FICFORGE POLICY</span>
    <h1>Content Policy</h1>
    <p>
      FicForge supports broad creative freedom while requiring accurate ratings, clear warnings, lawful publishing, and responsible community conduct.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-content-actions">
      <a href="/support/">← Return to Support</a>
      <a class="primary" href="/moderation-appeals/">Moderation & Appeals</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-content-intro">
    <span>OUR APPROACH</span>
    <h2>Creative freedom with clear boundaries</h2>
    <p>
      This platform  is built for fanfiction writers, original webnovel authors, and serialized storytellers. We support mature themes, dark fiction, political fiction, horror, romance, action, and complex storytelling, but creators must label their work honestly and avoid unlawful or harmful content. We want to ensure all get the point and follow the rules.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-content-grid">
    <a class="ff-content-card" href="#allowed-content">
      <strong>Allowed Content</strong>
      <p>Creative works that are lawful, properly rated, and clearly tagged.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-content-card" href="#required-warnings">
      <strong>Required Warnings</strong>
      <p>Major themes and sensitive content that must be disclosed.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-content-card" href="#ratings">
      <strong>Ratings System</strong>
      <p>General, Teen, Mature, and Explicit rating expectations.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-content-card" href="#explicit-rules">
      <strong>Explicit Content Rules</strong>
      <p>Age-gating and monetization limits for explicit content.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-content-card" href="#fanfiction-rules">
      <strong>Fanfiction Boundaries</strong>
      <p>Fanfiction remains free and separate from direct story sales.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-content-card" href="#prohibited-content">
      <strong>Prohibited Content</strong>
      <p>Content and behavior that may lead to removal or bans.</p>
    </a>
  </div>

  <div id="allowed-content" class="ff-content-panel">
    <span>ALLOWED CONTENT</span>
    <h2>What FicForge allows</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge allows a wide range of fiction, fanfiction, original webnovels, serialized stories, and mature creative themes as long as the content remains lawful, properly labeled, and compliant with platform rules.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-content-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Fanfiction</strong>
        <p>Transformative stories based on existing fandoms, worlds, characters, or settings, subject to FicForge’s fanfiction boundaries.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Original Fiction</strong>
        <p>Creator-owned stories, worlds, characters, and webnovels written from original intellectual property.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Mature Themes</strong>
        <p>Stories may explore war, politics, trauma, horror, romance, grief, violence, social conflict, or morally complex characters when properly rated.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Dark Fiction</strong>
        <p>Dark stories are allowed when they remain fictional, properly warned, and do not encourage real-world harm.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="required-warnings" class="ff-content-panel">
    <span>REQUIRED WARNINGS</span>
    <h2>Creators must warn readers honestly</h2>
    <p>
      Content warnings help readers make informed choices. FicForge does not require every small detail to be spoiled, but major sensitive content should be clearly labeled.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-content-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Graphic Violence</strong>
        <p>Use warnings for intense violence, gore, torture, battlefield brutality, or repeated violent imagery.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Sexual Content</strong>
        <p>Use the correct rating and warning when a story contains sexual themes, sexual situations, or explicit adult material.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Abuse or Trauma Themes</strong>
        <p>Warn for abuse, coercion, trauma, captivity, exploitation themes, or similar sensitive material.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Self-Harm or Suicide Themes</strong>
        <p>Stories involving self-harm, suicidal ideation, or related themes should be clearly warned.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Major Character Death</strong>
        <p>Use warnings when major character death is a central or serious part of the story.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Extreme Horror</strong>
        <p>Warn for extreme horror, psychological horror, body horror, or disturbing imagery that may affect readers.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="ratings" class="ff-content-panel">
    <span>RATINGS SYSTEM</span>
    <h2>Choose the rating that fits the strongest content</h2>
    <p>
      Ratings should reflect the strongest material in your story, not just the average chapter. Under-rating content may lead to correction requests or moderation action.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-rating-grid">
      <div>
        <strong>General</strong>
        <p>Suitable for most readers. Little to no intense violence, sexual content, or heavy mature themes.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Teen</strong>
        <p>May include mild violence, light romance, moderate language, or themes appropriate for older teen readers.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Mature</strong>
        <p>May include heavy profanity, intense themes, stronger action violence, trauma, political darkness, or fade-to-black sexual content.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Explicit</strong>
        <p>For adult audiences only. Applies to highly graphic sexual detail, prolonged anatomical sexual description, extreme gore, or extreme visceral torture.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="explicit-rules" class="ff-content-panel">
    <span>EXPLICIT CONTENT</span>
    <h2>Explicit content has stricter platform limits</h2>
    <p>
      Explicit-rated stories must be clearly labeled and age-gated. FicForge may require an 18+ gateway before readers access Explicit content.
    </p>
    <p>
      Explicit content cannot use Sparks, Fast Pass, internal profile-support triggers, paid chapter access, or other FicForge monetization tools. Explicit stories must remain free to access where permitted by platform rules and payment processor requirements.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div id="fanfiction-rules" class="ff-content-panel">
    <span>FANFICTION MONETIZATION BOUNDARIES</span>
    <h2>FicForge does not sell fanfiction</h2>
    <p>
      Fanfiction chapters on FicForge remain free to read. They cannot be sold, locked, placed behind Fast Pass, offered as paid early access, or used as payment rewards.
    </p>
    <p>
      General profile support, where available, is for the creator’s overall public presence on FicForge. It is not attached to any specific fanfiction story, fandom, chapter, update, continuation, ship, character, bonus scene, or reward.
    </p>
    <p>
      Creators may not use FicForge profile support, author notes, story descriptions, comments, or platform tools to promise fanfiction rewards in exchange for payment.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div id="prohibited-content" class="ff-content-panel warning">
    <span>PROHIBITED CONTENT</span>
    <h2>Content that is not allowed on FicForge</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge may remove content, restrict accounts, suspend users, or permanently ban accounts when serious violations occur.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-content-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Illegal Exploitation</strong>
        <p>Any illegal exploitative material, especially involving minors, is strictly prohibited and may be reported where required.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Plagiarism and Scraping</strong>
        <p>Stolen work, unauthorized reposting, scraping, compilation theft, or impersonation of another creator is prohibited.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Doxxing and Harassment</strong>
        <p>Threats, stalking, targeted harassment, coordinated abuse, or sharing private personal information is not allowed.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Hate or Real-World Harm</strong>
        <p>Content or behavior that promotes real-world violence, targeted hate, extremist recruitment, or direct harm is prohibited.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Fraud or Platform Abuse</strong>
        <p>Fake accounts, payment fraud, manipulation, spam, malicious links, or abuse of platform systems may lead to enforcement.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Copyright Abuse</strong>
        <p>Repeated infringement, ignoring takedown rules, or reposting removed material may lead to account termination.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-content-panel">
    <span>COMMUNITY CONDUCT</span>
    <h2>Respect readers, writers, and moderators</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge communities should form naturally around stories. Discussion, criticism, theories, reviews, and disagreement are allowed, but harassment, threats, stalking, slurs, coordinated attacks, and malicious behavior are not.
    </p>
    <p>
      Users are expected to follow platform rules in comments, profiles, story descriptions, author notes, community areas, and support interactions.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-content-bottom">
    <h2>Need to report a problem?</h2>
    <p>
      Use Contact Support for bugs, account issues, plagiarism reports, content concerns, copyright questions, or moderation problems. More detailed rules are available through DMCA & Copyright and Moderation & Appeals.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-content-actions">
      <a class="primary" href="/contact/">Contact Support</a>
      <a href="/dmca-copyright/">DMCA & Copyright</a>
      <a href="/moderation-appeals/">Moderation & Appeals</a>
    </div>
  </div>
</section>

  <div class="ff-payout-hero">
    <span class="label">Creator Earnings</span>
    <h1>Creator Payout FAQ</h1>
    <p>
      Learn how creator earnings, Sparks conversion, payout thresholds, payout schedules, and monetization limits work on FicForge.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-actions">
      <a href="/support/">← Return to Support</a>
      <a class="primary" href="/creator-earnings/">Open Creator Earnings</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-alert">
    <strong>Important launch note</strong>
    <p>
      Creator payouts are part of FicForge’s planned monetization system. Some payout tools, payment methods, verification steps, and dashboards may launch in stages as the platform grows.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-grid">
    <div class="ff-payout-card">
      <strong>100 Sparks = $1.00</strong>
      <p>Creator earnings are calculated using FicForge’s creator-side Spark conversion rate.</p>
    </div>

    <div class="ff-payout-card">
      <strong>$50 Threshold</strong>
      <p>Creators must reach 5,000 eligible Sparks before requesting a payout.</p>
    </div>

    <div class="ff-payout-card">
      <strong>1st & 15th</strong>
      <p>Eligible payouts are planned for semi-monthly processing on the 1st and 15th.</p>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Basics</span>
    <h2>How creators earn on FicForge</h2>
    <p>
      Creators may earn through eligible creator-support features, Sparks-based support, and future original-webnovel monetization tools. Earnings depend on the type of content, platform rules, account eligibility, and payout availability.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-two">
      <div class="ff-payout-box">
        <strong>Original Webnovels</strong>
        <p>
          Original webnovel creators may become eligible for broader monetization features as FicForge grows, including creator support, future premium tools, and approved story-based monetization.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-payout-box">
        <strong>Fanfiction Creators</strong>
        <p>
          Fanfiction chapters cannot be sold, locked, Fast Passed, or offered as paid early access. Support connected to fanfiction creators must remain general profile support, not payment for a specific fanfic.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Spark Conversion</span>
    <h2>How Sparks convert into creator earnings</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge uses a creator-side Spark conversion rate to calculate eligible creator earnings.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-highlight">
      <p>
        <strong>Creator conversion:</strong> 100 Sparks = $1.00<br>
        <strong>Payout threshold:</strong> 5,000 Sparks = $50.00<br>
        <strong>Minimum payout:</strong> Creators must reach the payout threshold before requesting withdrawal.
      </p>
    </div>

    <p style="margin-top:14px;">
      The consumer price of Sparks may differ from the creator conversion rate because purchase pricing may account for platform operations, payment processing fees, fraud prevention, refunds, taxes, and service costs.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Payout Threshold</span>
    <h2>When creators can request a payout</h2>
    <p>
      A creator must reach the minimum payout threshold before a payout request can be processed.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Minimum threshold</strong>
        <p>Creators must have at least 5,000 eligible Sparks, equal to $50.00 in creator earnings, before requesting a payout.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Eligible balance</strong>
        <p>Only cleared and eligible earnings count toward payout. Pending, disputed, refunded, reversed, or held earnings may not be available for withdrawal.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Unreached threshold</strong>
        <p>If a creator has not reached the threshold, the balance remains in the creator account until enough eligible earnings are available.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Payout Schedule</span>
    <h2>When payouts are processed</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge plans to process eligible creator payouts semi-monthly.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Processing days</strong>
        <p>Eligible payouts are planned for the 1st and 15th of each month.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Review time</strong>
        <p>Payouts may require review before processing, especially for new creators, unusual activity, copyright reports, policy concerns, refund issues, or account verification problems.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Bank/payment delays</strong>
        <p>After FicForge processes a payout, the final arrival time may depend on the payout provider, bank, region, holidays, and account status.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Payment Methods</span>
    <h2>How creators may receive payouts</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge may support multiple payout methods as the platform develops. Availability may depend on the creator’s country, account verification, payment processor rules, and platform rollout stage.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Possible payout methods</strong>
        <p>FicForge may support Stripe Connect, PayPal Payouts, bank transfer, direct transfer, or other approved payment methods in the future.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Verification may be required</strong>
        <p>Creators may need to verify identity, payment account ownership, tax information, region eligibility, or other required details before receiving payouts.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Availability may vary</strong>
        <p>Not every payout method will be available to every creator immediately. FicForge may roll out payout options in stages.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Fanfiction Limits</span>
    <h2>Fanfiction creator support rules</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge allows fanfiction as community storytelling, but fanfiction monetization must be handled carefully.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Fanfiction chapters remain free</strong>
        <p>Fanfiction chapters cannot be sold, locked behind payment, placed behind Fast Pass, offered as paid early access, or used as paid rewards.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Profile support must stay general</strong>
        <p>Readers may support a creator’s overall public profile as general appreciation for that creator’s presence on FicForge, not for any specific fanfiction story, chapter, fandom, update, continuation, ship, character, bonus scene, or reward.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>No paid fanfiction promises</strong>
        <p>Creators may not promise fanfiction updates, bonus chapters, continuations, private chapters, commissions, or special fandom rewards in exchange for Sparks, subscriptions, tips, donations, or outside payments.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Original Fiction</span>
    <h2>Original webnovel monetization</h2>
    <p>
      Original webnovels are treated differently from fanfiction because they are based on the creator’s own characters, setting, world, and core concept.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Eligible original works</strong>
        <p>Original webnovel creators may become eligible for story-based monetization features when those tools are available and their account meets platform requirements.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Platform share</strong>
        <p>FicForge may keep a platform share from eligible original-fiction monetization to support hosting, tools, payment processing, moderation, and platform operations.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Creator responsibility</strong>
        <p>Creators are responsible for making sure their original work does not copy, plagiarize, or infringe another person’s protected work.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Holds & Reviews</span>
    <h2>Why earnings or payouts may be held</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge may delay, hold, reduce, reverse, or deny payouts when required for safety, payment review, copyright enforcement, fraud prevention, or legal compliance.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Payment disputes or refunds</strong>
        <p>Earnings connected to refunded, disputed, charged back, reversed, or suspicious transactions may be held or removed from the eligible payout balance.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Copyright or plagiarism reports</strong>
        <p>Accounts under serious copyright, plagiarism, reposting, or impersonation review may have earnings held while FicForge reviews the issue.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Policy violations</strong>
        <p>Content Policy violations, ban evasion, fake engagement, payment abuse, or misleading monetization may affect payout eligibility.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Verification problems</strong>
        <p>Payouts may be delayed if creator identity, payout method, tax information, account ownership, or regional eligibility cannot be verified.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Taxes & Responsibility</span>
    <h2>Creator tax responsibility</h2>
    <p>
      Creators are responsible for understanding and handling any taxes, reporting duties, local rules, or legal obligations connected to their earnings.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-list">
      <div>
        <strong>FicForge does not provide tax advice</strong>
        <p>Information on this page is general platform guidance and should not be treated as tax, financial, or legal advice.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Forms may be required later</strong>
        <p>Depending on region, payout provider, and earnings activity, creators may need to provide tax forms or payment verification before receiving payouts.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Creator records</strong>
        <p>Creators should keep their own records of earnings, payouts, fees, refunds, and platform statements.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-panel">
    <span class="label">Related Pages</span>
    <h2>Where to go next</h2>
    <p>
      This page focuses only on payout rules. Use the pages below for related topics.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-links">
      <a href="/sparks-faq/">Sparks FAQ</a>
      <a href="/protection-rights/">Protection & Rights</a>
      <a href="/content-policy/">Content Policy</a>
      <a href="/dmca-copyright/">DMCA & Copyright</a>
      <a href="/creator-earnings/">Creator Earnings</a>
      <a href="/contact/">Contact Support</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-payout-bottom">
    <h2>Need help with earnings?</h2>
    <p>
      Visit Creator Earnings to review your payout status, or contact FicForge Support if you believe your balance, payout request, or monetization status is incorrect.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-payout-actions">
      <a class="primary" href="/creator-earnings/">Open Creator Earnings</a>
      <a href="/contact/">Contact Support</a>
    </div>
  </div>
</section>

  <div class="ff-dmca-hero">
    <span class="label">Copyright Center</span>
    <h1>DMCA & Copyright</h1>
    <p>
      FicForge respects intellectual property rights and provides a process for copyright holders, creators, and users to report copyright concerns, plagiarism, reposted work, and mistaken removals.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-actions">
      <a href="/support/">← Return to Support</a>
      <a class="primary" href="mailto:dmca@ficforge.org?subject=DMCA%20Copyright%20Notice%20-%20FicForge">Email DMCA Agent</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-alert">
    <strong>Important Notice</strong>
    <p>
      This page explains FicForge’s copyright reporting process. It is not legal advice. Copyright notices and counter-notices can have legal consequences, so users should only submit truthful, accurate, good-faith claims.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-grid">
    <div class="ff-dmca-card">
      <strong>Copyright Notices</strong>
      <p>Report content that you believe infringes your copyright or has been reposted without permission.</p>
    </div>

    <div class="ff-dmca-card">
      <strong>Counter-Notices</strong>
      <p>Creators may respond if they believe their content was removed because of mistake or misidentification.</p>
    </div>

    <div class="ff-dmca-card">
      <strong>Repeat Infringers</strong>
      <p>Accounts that repeatedly violate copyright rules may lose publishing access, monetization, or their account.</p>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Our Position</span>
    <h2>FicForge respects copyright</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge is a user-generated storytelling platform. Creators are responsible for the content they upload, publish, or share through their accounts. FicForge does not allow users to upload stolen works, repost another creator’s writing without permission, impersonate copyright owners, or claim ownership of content they did not create.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-two">
      <div class="ff-dmca-box">
        <strong>Original Works</strong>
        <p>
          Creators keep ownership of their original stories, characters, settings, and creative writing, subject to the limited permissions needed for FicForge to host, display, distribute, and operate the platform.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-dmca-box">
        <strong>Fanfiction Works</strong>
        <p>
          Fanfiction may involve third-party worlds, characters, fandoms, or source material. FicForge does not claim ownership of those third-party properties, and creators are responsible for respecting applicable copyright rules.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Designated Agent</span>
    <h2>Email FicForge’s DMCA agent</h2>
    <p>
      Copyright owners or authorized representatives may submit copyright notices to FicForge’s designated copyright agent by email. Please include all required information listed below so the report can be reviewed properly.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-contact">
      <p>
        <strong>DMCA Email:</strong>
<a href="mailto:dmca@ficforge.org?subject=DMCA%20Copyright%20Notice%20-%20FicForge">dmca@ficforge.org</a><br>
<strong>Designated Agent:</strong> FicForge DMCA Agent<br>
<strong>Service Provider:</strong> FicForge<br>
<strong>Mailing Address:</strong> To be updated after official designated-agent registration<br>
<strong>Phone:</strong> To be updated after official designated-agent registration
      </p>
    </div>

    <p style="margin-top:14px;">
      After FicForge completes its designated-agent registration, this section will be updated with the official registered agent information.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Takedown Notices</span>
    <h2>How to submit a copyright notice</h2>
    <p>
      To request removal of content you believe infringes your copyright, send a written notice to FicForge’s designated copyright agent. The notice should be clear, complete, and specific enough for FicForge to identify the content and review the claim.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-list">
      <div>
        <strong>1. Your contact information</strong>
        <p>Include your full legal name, organization if applicable, mailing address, phone number, and email address.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>2. The copyrighted work</strong>
        <p>Identify the copyrighted work you believe has been infringed. For multiple works, provide a representative list or clear description.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>3. The allegedly infringing content</strong>
        <p>Provide the FicForge URL, story title, chapter title, username, screenshots if helpful, and any other information that allows FicForge to locate the material.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>4. Good-faith statement</strong>
        <p>State that you have a good-faith belief that the disputed use is not authorized by the copyright owner, its agent, or the law.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>5. Accuracy and authority statement</strong>
        <p>State that the information in the notice is accurate and that you are the copyright owner or authorized to act on behalf of the copyright owner.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>6. Signature</strong>
        <p>Include a physical or electronic signature of the copyright owner or authorized representative.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Review Process</span>
    <h2>What happens after FicForge receives a notice</h2>
    <p>
      After receiving a copyright notice, FicForge may review the report, remove or disable access to the reported material, notify the affected creator, request additional information, or take further action when needed.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Content removal or access restriction</strong>
        <p>Reported material may be removed or disabled while the copyright issue is reviewed.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Creator notification</strong>
        <p>FicForge may notify the creator whose content was removed or disabled and provide information about the claim where appropriate.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Account review</strong>
        <p>FicForge may review the creator’s account history, prior notices, reposting behavior, monetization activity, and related reports.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Further action</strong>
        <p>Depending on the situation, FicForge may restrict publishing, suspend monetization, remove content, suspend accounts, or terminate repeat infringer accounts.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Counter-Notices</span>
    <h2>How to submit a counter-notice</h2>
    <p>
      If your content was removed or disabled because of a copyright notice and you believe the removal was caused by mistake or misidentification, you may submit a counter-notice to FicForge’s designated copyright agent.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-list">
      <div>
        <strong>1. Identify the removed material</strong>
        <p>Provide the story title, chapter title, URL if available, username, and a description of the material that was removed or disabled.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>2. Provide your contact information</strong>
        <p>Include your full legal name, mailing address, phone number, and email address.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>3. Good-faith mistake statement</strong>
        <p>State that you have a good-faith belief that the material was removed or disabled because of mistake or misidentification.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>4. Consent to jurisdiction</strong>
        <p>Include the required consent to the jurisdiction of the appropriate federal district court, or another applicable jurisdiction statement if required by law.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>5. Acceptance of service</strong>
        <p>State that you will accept service of process from the person who submitted the original notice or their authorized agent.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>6. Signature</strong>
        <p>Include a physical or electronic signature.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Restoration</span>
    <h2>What happens after a counter-notice</h2>
    <p>
      After receiving a valid counter-notice, FicForge may forward it to the original complaining party. If the complaining party does not notify FicForge that they have filed a court action seeking to restrain the disputed activity, FicForge may restore the removed or disabled material after the legally required waiting period.
    </p>

    <p style="margin-top:12px;">
      Submitting a counter-notice is a serious legal step. Do not submit one casually, dishonestly, or without understanding the possible consequences.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Plagiarism & Reposting</span>
    <h2>Reports that are not formal DMCA notices</h2>
    <p>
      Some reports may involve plagiarism, reposted writing, copied summaries, stolen cover art, impersonation, or creator disputes that do not arrive as formal DMCA notices. FicForge may still review these reports under platform rules.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Examples of reportable behavior</strong>
        <p>Reposting another creator’s chapters, copying an original story without permission, uploading stolen cover art, impersonating an author, or falsely claiming ownership of another person’s work.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Helpful evidence</strong>
        <p>Provide links to the original work, links to the copied work, screenshots, upload dates, usernames, and a clear explanation of what was copied.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Platform action</strong>
        <p>FicForge may remove content, warn users, restrict features, suspend publishing, or take account action depending on the severity and evidence.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Repeat Infringers</span>
    <h2>Repeat copyright violations</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge may take stronger action against users who repeatedly upload infringing material, repost other creators’ work, ignore copyright warnings, evade removals, or create new accounts to continue violating copyright rules.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Possible consequences</strong>
        <p>Content removal, publishing restrictions, account warnings, monetization suspension, payout review, account suspension, or permanent account termination.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Monetization impact</strong>
        <p>Creators involved in copyright abuse, reposting, or plagiarism may lose access to Sparks, payouts, premium creator tools, or other monetized features.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Ban evasion</strong>
        <p>Creating a new account to avoid copyright enforcement may result in additional removals, account restrictions, or termination.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">False Claims</span>
    <h2>Do not submit false or abusive notices</h2>
    <p>
      Copyright notices and counter-notices must be truthful. Submitting false, misleading, abusive, or bad-faith copyright claims may have legal consequences and may also result in FicForge account action.
    </p>

    <p style="margin-top:12px;">
      Do not use the DMCA process to harass creators, silence criticism, remove content you simply dislike, attack competitors, or make claims over works you do not own or represent.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Fanfiction Boundary</span>
    <h2>Fanfiction and third-party rights</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge allows fanfiction as transformative community storytelling, but fanfiction may involve third-party intellectual property. FicForge does not own those third-party worlds, characters, settings, or source materials.
    </p>

    <p style="margin-top:12px;">
      Fanfiction creators remain responsible for following copyright law, FicForge rules, and any applicable rights-holder restrictions. FicForge may remove or restrict fanfiction content if required by a valid copyright notice, platform policy, payment processor concern, or legal obligation.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-panel">
    <span class="label">Related Pages</span>
    <h2>Where to go next</h2>
    <p>
      To avoid repeating the same information across the site, this page focuses only on copyright and DMCA procedures. Use the pages below for other rules and support topics.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-links">
      <a href="/protection-rights/">Protection & Rights</a>
      <a href="/content-policy/">Content Policy</a>
      <a href="/creator-payout-faq/">Creator Payout FAQ</a>
      <a href="/sparks-faq/">Sparks FAQ</a>
      <a href="/moderation-appeals/">Moderation & Appeals</a>
      <a href="/contact/">Contact Support</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-dmca-bottom">
    <h2>Need to report a copyright issue?</h2>
    <p>
      Email FicForge’s DMCA agent with a clear report, links to the content, and enough detail for FicForge to identify the material, understand the claim, and take appropriate action.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-dmca-actions">
      <a class="primary" href="mailto:dmca@ficforge.org?subject=DMCA%20Copyright%20Notice%20-%20FicForge">Email DMCA Agent</a>
      <a href="/support/">Return to Support</a>
    </div>
  </div>
</section>

  <div class="ff-guides-hero">
    <span class="label">CREATOR GUIDES</span>
    <h1>Guides & Resources</h1>
    <p>
      Practical guides for preparing, formatting, and publishing stories on FicForge. This page helps new creators understand what to prepare before posting their first work.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guides-actions">
      <a href="/support/">← Return to Support</a>
      <a class="primary" href="/my-stories/">Open My Stories</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-guides-intro">
    <span class="label">START HERE</span>
    <h2>Prepare your story before publishing</h2>
    <p>
      A strong story page helps readers understand what your work is, where it belongs, and why they should keep reading. Before publishing, prepare your title, synopsis, story type, fandom or genre, tags, rating, warnings, cover image, and first chapter.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-guides-grid">
    <a class="ff-guide-card" href="#story-checklist">
      <strong>Story Checklist</strong>
      <p>What to prepare before using the Create Story form.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-guide-card" href="#story-type">
      <strong>Story Type</strong>
      <p>Choose Fanfiction or Original Webnovel correctly.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-guide-card" href="#synopsis">
      <strong>Synopsis Guide</strong>
      <p>Write a clear hook that helps readers start reading.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-guide-card" href="#metadata">
      <strong>Genres, Fandoms & Tags</strong>
      <p>Use metadata so readers can find your story.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-guide-card" href="#ratings">
      <strong>Ratings & Warnings</strong>
      <p>Label your strongest content honestly.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-guide-card" href="#covers">
      <strong>Cover Images</strong>
      <p>Prepare a readable cover for your story listing.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-guide-card" href="#formatting">
      <strong>Chapter Formatting</strong>
      <p>Make your chapters clean and comfortable to read.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-guide-card" href="#publishing">
      <strong>Publishing Habits</strong>
      <p>Build consistency and trust with readers.</p>
    </a>
  </div>

  <div id="story-checklist" class="ff-guide-panel">
    <span class="label">STORY CHECKLIST</span>
    <h2>What to prepare before creating a story</h2>
    <p>
      The Create Story form works best when your main story details are ready before you begin.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guide-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Story Title</strong>
        <p>Choose a clear, memorable title that is easy to read and not overloaded with extra symbols.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Synopsis</strong>
        <p>Prepare a short description that explains the premise, main conflict, tone, and reason readers should care.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Story Type</strong>
        <p>Select whether your work is Fanfiction or Original Webnovel so FicForge can organize it properly.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>First Chapter</strong>
        <p>Have your opening chapter ready. The first chapter should give readers a reason to continue.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="story-type" class="ff-guide-panel">
    <span class="label">STORY TYPE</span>
    <h2>Choosing Fanfiction or Original Webnovel</h2>
    <p>
      Story type tells FicForge how your work should be organized.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guide-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Fanfiction</strong>
        <p>Choose Fanfiction when your story uses an existing fandom, world, characters, setting, or source material. Add the fandom name clearly so readers can find it.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Original Webnovel</strong>
        <p>Choose Original Webnovel when your story is built from your own original characters, setting, world, and core concept.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="synopsis" class="ff-guide-panel">
    <span class="label">SYNOPSIS GUIDE</span>
    <h2>Writing a stronger synopsis</h2>
    <p>
      Your synopsis should tell readers what kind of journey they are entering without revealing every major twist.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guide-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Start with the premise</strong>
        <p>Explain the main idea of the story in plain language. Readers should understand the setup quickly.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Show the conflict</strong>
        <p>Let readers know what pressure, danger, romance, mystery, war, rivalry, or personal struggle drives the story.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Make the tone clear</strong>
        <p>Signal whether the story is dark, comedic, romantic, political, action-heavy, tragic, slow-burn, or adventurous.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Avoid empty hype</strong>
        <p>Instead of saying your story is “epic,” describe what actually makes it interesting.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="metadata" class="ff-guide-panel">
    <span class="label">DISCOVERY</span>
    <h2>Genres, fandoms, and tags</h2>
    <p>
      Accurate metadata helps the right readers find your story.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guide-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Genres</strong>
        <p>Use broad categories such as Fantasy, Science Fiction, Romance, Horror, Mystery, Action, Adventure, Historical, LitRPG, Drama, War, or Political Intrigue.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Fandoms</strong>
        <p>For fanfiction, name the main fandom clearly. For crossovers, include the major fandoms without overcrowding the field.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Tags</strong>
        <p>Use tags for tropes, tone, relationships, setting, pacing, and major elements like Reincarnation, Slow Burn, Kingdom Building, or Time Travel.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Accuracy Matters</strong>
        <p>Do not use popular tags that do not apply. Misleading tags damage reader trust.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="ratings" class="ff-guide-panel">
    <span class="label">RATINGS & WARNINGS</span>
    <h2>Help readers choose safely</h2>
    <p>
      Ratings and warnings help readers decide what they are comfortable reading.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guide-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Pick the honest rating</strong>
        <p>Choose the rating based on the strongest content in the story, not the average chapter.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Use warnings for major content</strong>
        <p>Warn for major themes such as graphic violence, sexual content, abuse themes, self-harm themes, major character death, or extreme horror.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Update when needed</strong>
        <p>When your story grows darker or more intense, update the rating and warnings.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Use the Content Policy</strong>
        <p>For detailed rules about Mature, Explicit, prohibited content, and safety boundaries, check the Content Policy page.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="covers" class="ff-guide-panel">
    <span class="label">COVER IMAGES</span>
    <h2>Make your story cover readable</h2>
    <p>
      A cover should be clean, readable, and suited to the story’s tone.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guide-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Readable Title</strong>
        <p>Make sure the title can still be read when the cover appears smaller on mobile or in story lists.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Match the Genre</strong>
        <p>A horror story, romance, war epic, and sci-fi adventure should not all feel visually identical.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Use Permitted Images</strong>
        <p>Use your own art, licensed assets, public-domain materials, or images you have permission to use.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Simple Is Fine</strong>
        <p>A clean text-based cover is better than a crowded image that looks messy or unreadable.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="formatting" class="ff-guide-panel">
    <span class="label">CHAPTER FORMATTING</span>
    <h2>Make chapters comfortable to read</h2>
    <p>
      Clean formatting keeps readers immersed in the story.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guide-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Break paragraphs clearly</strong>
        <p>Avoid giant walls of text. Break paragraphs naturally around dialogue, action, description, and emotional beats.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Preview after pasting</strong>
        <p>When copying from Google Docs or Microsoft Word, preview the chapter to make sure spacing and line breaks still look right.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Use author notes carefully</strong>
        <p>Keep notes helpful and brief. Avoid interrupting the middle of the chapter with unrelated announcements.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Think mobile first</strong>
        <p>Many readers will use phones. Shorter paragraphs and clean spacing make long chapters easier to read.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="publishing" class="ff-guide-panel">
    <span class="label">PUBLISHING HABITS</span>
    <h2>Build reader trust over time</h2>
    <p>
      Strong creators build trust through consistency, clean presentation, and honest communication.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guide-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Set realistic update goals</strong>
        <p>A steady weekly or biweekly schedule is often better than promising daily chapters and burning out.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Keep story details updated</strong>
        <p>Update your synopsis, tags, rating, warnings, and status when the story changes direction.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Respect reader expectations</strong>
        <p>Readers should not feel tricked by misleading tags, hidden warnings, or false update promises.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Use support pages</strong>
        <p>For deeper rules about copyright, content limits, creator rights, Sparks, payouts, or moderation, use the dedicated Support pages.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-guides-bottom">
    <h2>Ready to publish?</h2>
    <p>
      Open My Stories when you are ready to create your first story, upload a cover, choose your story type, add ratings and warnings, and publish your first chapter.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-guides-actions">
      <a class="primary" href="/my-stories/">Open My Stories</a>
      <a href="/help-center/">Visit Help Center</a>
    </div>
  </div>
</section>

PAGE: Help Center  (slug: help-center)

<section class="ff-help-page">
  <div class="ff-help-hero">
    <span>FICFORGE HELP CENTER</span>
    <h1>How can we help?</h1>
    <p>
      Find answers for account setup, reading, publishing, story management, creator tools, reports, Sparks, and general FicForge support.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-help-actions">
      <a href="/support/">← Return to Support</a>
      <a class="primary" href="/contact/">Contact Support</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-help-search">
    <span>START HERE</span>
    <h2>Choose the help area that matches your issue</h2>
    <p>
      The Help Center is built for quick answers. Deeper policy pages such as Content Policy, DMCA & Copyright, Creator Payout FAQ, Sparks FAQ, and Moderation & Appeals will explain the more serious platform rules in detail.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-help-grid">
    <a class="ff-help-card" href="#account-help">
      <strong>Account Help</strong>
      <p>Login issues, passwords, email changes, profiles, and account access.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-help-card" href="#reader-help">
      <strong>Reader Help</strong>
      <p>Finding stories, following creators, saving works, comments, and reader tools.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-help-card" href="#creator-help">
      <strong>Creator Help</strong>
      <p>Publishing stories, using the writer portal, uploading covers, and managing work.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-help-card" href="#story-setup">
      <strong>Story Setup</strong>
      <p>Titles, synopsis, story type, fandoms, genres, tags, ratings, and warnings.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-help-card" href="#payments-support">
      <strong>Sparks & Payments</strong>
      <p>Basic help with Sparks, future payouts, profile support, and creator earnings.</p>
    </a>

    <a class="ff-help-card" href="#reports">
      <strong>Reports & Safety</strong>
      <p>Reporting bugs, content issues, copyright concerns, abuse, or moderation problems.</p>
    </a>
  </div>

  <div id="account-help" class="ff-help-panel">
    <span>ACCOUNT HELP</span>
    <h2>Accounts, login, and profile basics</h2>

    <div class="ff-faq-list">
      <div>
        <strong>I cannot log in. What should I do?</strong>
        <p>
          Use the password reset option on the login page first. Make sure you are using the same email address connected to your FicForge account. If the reset email does not arrive, check spam or contact support.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Can readers become writers?</strong>
        <p>
          Yes. FicForge is designed so readers can become creators without needing a separate platform. A user can read, follow stories, comment, and also open the Writer Portal when they are ready to publish.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Where do I manage my writer tools?</strong>
        <p>
          Go to the Writer Portal or My Stories page. These areas are used for story creation, submitted stories, drafts, scheduled release tools, creator analytics placeholders, earnings, and support links.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="reader-help" class="ff-help-panel">
    <span>READER HELP</span>
    <h2>Reading, following, and discovering stories</h2>

    <div class="ff-faq-list">
      <div>
        <strong>How do I find stories?</strong>
        <p>
          Use Browse, fandom categories, genre categories, tags, search, and future story filters. FicForge will organize fanfiction and original webnovels clearly so readers can find the type of story they want.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Will chapters have ads in the middle?</strong>
        <p>
          No. FicForge is built around a stories-first reading experience. Ads should not interrupt the body of a story chapter. Any future ad placements should remain outside the actual chapter text.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Can I comment on stories?</strong>
        <p>
          FicForge is planned around reader discussion beneath stories and chapters. Comment tools may develop over time, but the goal is to let communities form naturally around the stories they read.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="creator-help" class="ff-help-panel">
    <span>CREATOR HELP</span>
    <h2>Publishing and managing your stories</h2>

    <div class="ff-faq-list">
      <div>
        <strong>How do I publish a story?</strong>
        <p>
          Go to My Stories and use the Create Story form. Add your title, synopsis, story type, genre, fandom if needed, tags, rating, warnings, cover image, and first chapter.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>What is the difference between Fanfiction and Original Webnovel?</strong>
        <p>
          Fanfiction uses existing copyrighted worlds, fandoms, characters, or settings and must remain free to read. Original webnovels are creator-owned stories and may later access broader monetization tools.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Can I edit a story after publishing?</strong>
        <p>
          Story management tools will appear in My Stories. As the platform develops, creators should be able to review submitted stories, update details, manage drafts, and continue publishing chapters.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="story-setup" class="ff-help-panel">
    <span>STORY SETUP</span>
    <h2>Titles, tags, ratings, and warnings</h2>

    <div class="ff-faq-list">
      <div>
        <strong>What should my synopsis include?</strong>
        <p>
          A strong synopsis should explain the core premise, main conflict, tone, and reason readers should care. Keep it clear, direct, and interesting without spoiling every major twist.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Why do ratings and warnings matter?</strong>
        <p>
          Ratings and warnings help readers choose stories safely. Creators are responsible for tagging major content concerns such as violence, explicit themes, abuse, self-harm themes, major character death, or other sensitive material.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>What tags should I use?</strong>
        <p>
          Use tags that help readers understand your story’s genre, tone, tropes, fandom, relationship focus, setting, and major themes. Avoid misleading tags or stuffing unrelated tags into your story.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="payments-support" class="ff-help-panel">
    <span>SPARKS & PAYMENTS</span>
    <h2>Basic Sparks, support, and payout help</h2>

    <div class="ff-faq-list">
      <div>
        <strong>What are Sparks?</strong>
        <p>
          Sparks are FicForge’s planned platform currency. Readers may eventually use Sparks for approved platform features such as supporting creators or unlocking eligible original webnovel Fast Pass chapters.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Can fanfiction use Sparks?</strong>
        <p>
          Fanfiction chapters cannot be sold, locked, or placed behind Fast Pass.  Readers may only support a creator’s overall public profile as general appreciation for that creator’s time, talent, and broader writing activity. This support is not payment for any specific fanfiction story, chapter, fandom, update, continuation, bonus scene, or reward.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Where can I learn more about payouts?</strong>
        <p>
          The Creator Payout FAQ will explain creator earnings, payout thresholds, payout timing, verification requirements, and payment method availability once those systems are connected.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div id="reports" class="ff-help-panel">
    <span>REPORTS & SAFETY</span>
    <h2>Reporting issues and getting help</h2>

    <div class="ff-faq-list">
      <div>
        <strong>How do I report plagiarism or stolen work?</strong>
        <p>
          Use the Contact Support page or the future report tools. Include the story link, creator name, copied material, and any evidence showing where the original work came from.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>How do copyright holders contact FicForge?</strong>
        <p>
          Copyright holders should use the DMCA & Copyright page once it is created. That page will explain takedown notice requirements, counter-notices, and repeat infringer rules.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>How do I report a bug?</strong>
        <p>
          Use Contact Support and describe what happened, what page you were on, what device or browser you used, and whether the issue happened more than once.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-help-bottom">
    <h2>Still need help?</h2>
    <p>
      Contact FicForge Support for account help, technical issues, story reports, copyright concerns, creator payout questions, or anything that is not answered here.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-help-actions">
      <a class="primary" href="/contact/">Contact Support</a>
      <a href="/support/">Return to Support Hub</a>
    </div>
  </div>
</section>

  <div class="ff-moderation-hero">
    <span class="label">Safety & Review</span>
    <h1>Moderation & Appeals</h1>
    <p>
      FicForge uses moderation to protect readers, creators, stories, payments, and the overall platform. This page explains how reports, enforcement actions, and appeals are handled.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-actions">
      <a href="/support/">← Return to Support</a>
      <a class="primary" href="/contact/">Submit a Report or Appeal</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-alert">
    <strong>Important launch note</strong>
    <p>
      During early launch, FicForge moderation may be handled manually through support forms and admin review. More advanced report tools, story-level report buttons, and automated moderation features may be added later.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-grid">
    <div class="ff-moderation-card">
      <strong>Reports</strong>
      <p>Users may report content, profiles, comments, plagiarism, impersonation, payment abuse, or safety concerns.</p>
    </div>

    <div class="ff-moderation-card">
      <strong>Reviews</strong>
      <p>FicForge may review reports, account activity, content history, evidence, and platform rules before taking action.</p>
    </div>

    <div class="ff-moderation-card">
      <strong>Appeals</strong>
      <p>Users may appeal certain warnings, removals, restrictions, suspensions, or account actions.</p>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-panel">
    <span class="label">Purpose</span>
    <h2>What moderation is for</h2>
    <p>
      Moderation exists to keep FicForge usable, safe, fair, and focused on stories. FicForge may review content or accounts when there are reports, policy concerns, copyright issues, payment risks, spam, harassment, impersonation, or signs of platform abuse.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-two">
      <div class="ff-moderation-box">
        <strong>Protecting users</strong>
        <p>
          Moderation helps protect readers and creators from harassment, spam, impersonation, stolen work, misleading monetization, and abusive behavior.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-moderation-box">
        <strong>Protecting the platform</strong>
        <p>
          FicForge may act when content, accounts, payments, or behavior create risk for the platform, creators, readers, payment systems, or hosting stability.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-panel">
    <span class="label">Reports</span>
    <h2>What users can report</h2>
    <p>
      Users may report issues that appear to violate FicForge rules, harm other users, abuse the platform, or misuse creator-support features.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Content concerns</strong>
        <p>Stories, chapters, profiles, comments, images, tags, summaries, warnings, or other material that may violate FicForge rules.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Copyright and plagiarism</strong>
        <p>Copied stories, reposted chapters, stolen cover art, impersonation, or content uploaded without permission.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Harassment and abuse</strong>
        <p>Threats, targeted harassment, hate, spam, brigading, evasion, manipulation, or abusive behavior toward users or staff.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Payment and Spark abuse</strong>
        <p>Suspicious transactions, fake support, refund abuse, chargebacks, monetization abuse, or attempts to sell restricted content.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-panel">
    <span class="label">Review Process</span>
    <h2>How FicForge reviews reports</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge may review reports manually, compare them against platform rules, check available evidence, and decide whether action is needed. Not every report will result in removal or account action.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-list">
      <div>
        <strong>1. Report received</strong>
        <p>A user submits a report through Contact Support, a report form, or another approved FicForge support channel.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>2. Evidence reviewed</strong>
        <p>FicForge may review URLs, screenshots, account history, story details, timestamps, previous reports, payment activity, or other available information.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>3. Rule check</strong>
        <p>The report is compared against FicForge’s Content Policy, DMCA & Copyright page, Sparks rules, payout rules, and community standards.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>4. Action or no action</strong>
        <p>FicForge may take action, request more information, issue a warning, remove content, restrict features, or decide that no violation was found.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-panel">
    <span class="label">Possible Actions</span>
    <h2>Moderation actions FicForge may take</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge may choose the action that fits the severity, evidence, history, and risk of the issue.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Content-level action</strong>
        <p>Content may be edited, age-restricted, hidden, unpublished, removed, reclassified, or required to add stronger ratings, warnings, tags, or disclaimers.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Account-level action</strong>
        <p>Users may receive warnings, temporary restrictions, publishing limits, comment restrictions, account suspension, or permanent account termination.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Monetization-level action</strong>
        <p>FicForge may pause creator support, hold earnings, delay payouts, suspend Sparks access, restrict monetized tools, or review payout eligibility.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Security-level action</strong>
        <p>FicForge may block spam, restrict suspicious accounts, investigate abuse patterns, or prevent users from evading previous enforcement.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-panel">
    <span class="label">Monetization Review</span>
    <h2>Payout holds and creator-support review</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge may hold or review creator earnings when there are serious policy concerns, copyright reports, payment disputes, suspicious activity, or account verification issues.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-highlight">
      <p>
        <strong>Platform fee rule:</strong> FicForge applies a 9% platform fee to eligible creator-support and monetized creator earnings. Creators keep 91% of eligible support value before payment-provider, refund, tax, or account-review issues that may apply.
      </p>
    </div>

    <div class="ff-moderation-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Why earnings may be held</strong>
        <p>Copyright complaints, plagiarism reports, chargebacks, refund abuse, suspicious Sparks activity, fake engagement, identity issues, or rule violations may trigger review.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>What may happen</strong>
        <p>Earnings may be delayed, held, reversed, reduced, removed from eligible balance, or blocked from payout depending on the situation.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Where payout rules live</strong>
        <p>Detailed payout thresholds, schedules, payment methods, and creator tax responsibility are explained on the Creator Payout FAQ page.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-panel">
    <span class="label">Copyright Enforcement</span>
    <h2>Copyright, plagiarism, and reposting enforcement</h2>
    <p>
      FicForge may remove or restrict content that appears to involve copyright infringement, plagiarism, reposting, impersonation, stolen cover art, or false ownership claims.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-list">
      <div>
        <strong>Formal copyright notices</strong>
        <p>Formal copyright complaints and counter-notices should be handled through the DMCA & Copyright process.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Platform plagiarism reports</strong>
        <p>Reports involving copied stories, reposted chapters, stolen summaries, or impersonation may be reviewed under FicForge platform rules even if they are not formal DMCA notices.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Repeat violations</strong>
        <p>Users who repeatedly upload infringing, copied, or stolen content may lose publishing access, monetization access, or their account.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-panel">
    <span class="label">Appeals</span>
    <h2>How to appeal a moderation decision</h2>
    <p>
      Users may appeal certain moderation decisions if they believe FicForge made a mistake, misunderstood the situation, or acted on incomplete information.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-list">
      <div>
        <strong>What can be appealed</strong>
        <p>Warnings, content removals, account restrictions, monetization holds, payout delays, suspensions, or other enforcement decisions may be eligible for review.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>What to include</strong>
        <p>Include your username, email, story or content URL, the action being appealed, a clear explanation, and any evidence that supports your appeal.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Be specific and honest</strong>
        <p>Appeals should explain why the decision was mistaken or why the issue has been corrected. Abusive, spammy, or dishonest appeals may be ignored or denied.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Appeal results</strong>
        <p>FicForge may uphold the decision, reverse it, reduce the action, request more information, restore content, or keep restrictions in place.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-panel">
    <span class="label">Report Abuse</span>
    <h2>Abuse of reports and appeals</h2>
    <p>
      Reports and appeals exist to solve real issues, not to harass creators, attack competitors, silence criticism, manipulate rankings, or overload support.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-list">
      <div>
        <strong>False reports</strong>
        <p>Repeated false, misleading, malicious, or bad-faith reports may lead to account restrictions or support limitations.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Appeal spam</strong>
        <p>Submitting the same appeal repeatedly without new information may delay review or result in the appeal being closed.</p>
      </div>

      <div>
        <strong>Retaliation</strong>
        <p>Using reports to target, threaten, or punish another user may be treated as harassment or platform abuse.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-panel">
    <span class="label">Related Pages</span>
    <h2>Where to go next</h2>
    <p>
      This page explains enforcement and appeals. Use the pages below for the detailed rules behind specific issues.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-links">
      <a href="/content-policy/">Content Policy</a>
      <a href="/dmca-copyright/">DMCA & Copyright</a>
      <a href="/creator-payout-faq/">Creator Payout FAQ</a>
      <a href="/sparks-faq/">Sparks FAQ</a>
      <a href="/protection-rights/">Protection & Rights</a>
      <a href="/contact/">Contact Support</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-moderation-bottom">
    <h2>Need to report or appeal something?</h2>
    <p>
      Use Contact Support to submit reports, appeals, copyright concerns, plagiarism evidence, payment concerns, or account-review questions.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-moderation-actions">
      <a class="primary" href="/contact/">Submit Report or Appeal</a>
      <a href="/support/">Return to Support</a>
    </div>
  </div>
</section>

        <strong>Creator-side conversion</strong>
        <p>
          For eligible creator support, 100 eligible Sparks equals $1.00 before FicForge’s 9% platform fee and before any payment-provider, refund, tax, or account-review issues.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-premium-panel">
    <span class="label">Important Boundaries</span>
    <h2>Premium and Sparks do not unlock fanfiction</h2>
    <p>
      Premium is a platform membership, and Sparks are platform currency. Neither should be used to sell restricted content or bypass FicForge rules.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-premium-two">
      <div class="ff-premium-box">
        <strong>Fanfiction remains free</strong>
        <p>
          Fanfiction chapters cannot be sold, locked behind Premium, offered as paid early access, placed behind Fast Pass, or used as paid rewards.
        </p>
      </div>

      <div class="ff-premium-box">
        <strong>General creator support only</strong>
        <p>
          Support connected to fanfiction creators must remain general profile support, not payment for a specific fanfiction story, chapter, fandom, update, continuation, bonus scene, or reward.
        </p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-premium-panel">
    <span class="label">Future Feature</span>
    <h2>Fast Pass is set aside for later</h2>
    <p>
      Fast Pass is planned as a future feature for eligible original webnovels only. It is not currently a Premium benefit, and it will not apply to fanfiction chapters.
    </p>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-premium-panel">
    <span class="label">Related Pages</span>
    <h2>Learn more</h2>
    <p>
      Use these pages for deeper details about Sparks, creator payouts, content rules, and creator rights.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-premium-links">
      <a href="/sparks-faq/">Sparks FAQ</a>
      <a href="/creator-payout-faq/">Creator Payout FAQ</a>
      <a href="/protection-rights/">Protection & Rights</a>
      <a href="/content-policy/">Content Policy</a>
      <a href="/support/">Support Hub</a>
      <a href="/contact/">Contact Support</a>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="ff-premium-bottom">
    <h2>Ready to support FicForge?</h2>
    <p>
      Choose a Premium membership for monthly Sparks and platform perks, or buy Sparks separately to support creators through approved FicForge features.
    </p>

    <div class="ff-premium-actions">
      <a class="primary" href="#sparks-store">View Spark Packs</a>
      <a href="/contact/">Contact Support</a>
